===== FILE: tests/browser/smoke_single.py (current text at 3d9bdad, line-numbered) =====
     1	"""Headless browser smoke test of the single-application screen.
     2	
     3	Checks: no console errors, no CSP violations, no failed requests; the three one-click samples reach
     4	their expected verdicts with crops and highlights; row selection highlights evidence; a phone-sized
     5	viewport does not scroll horizontally. Needs a running server and Playwright with Chromium.
     6	
     7	    LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/smoke_single.py
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	
    12	import json
    13	import os
    14	import sys
    15	import time
    16	import urllib.request
    17	
    18	from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright
    19	
    20	BASE = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")
    21	OUT = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_SHOTS", ".")
    22	EXPECTED = {"clean": "Ready for your approval", "photo": None, "problem": "Issues found"}
    23	
    24	
    25	def wait_ready(seconds: int = 120) -> None:
    26	    for _ in range(seconds):
    27	        try:
    28	            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{BASE}/api/v1/health", timeout=3) as r:
    29	                if json.load(r).get("ready"):
    30	                    return
    31	        except Exception:
    32	            pass
    33	        time.sleep(1)
    34	    sys.exit("server not ready")
    35	
    36	
    37	def main() -> int:
    38	    wait_ready()
    39	    problems: list[str] = []
    40	    with sync_playwright() as p:
    41	        browser = p.chromium.launch()
    42	        ctx = browser.new_context(viewport={"width": 1366, "height": 900})
    43	        page = ctx.new_page()
    44	        page.on(
    45	            "console",
    46	            lambda m: problems.append(f"console.{m.type}: {m.text}") if m.type in ("error", "warning") else None,
    47	        )
    48	        page.on("pageerror", lambda e: problems.append(f"pageerror: {e}"))
    49	        page.on("requestfailed", lambda r: problems.append(f"requestfailed: {r.url} {r.failure}"))
    50	        page.on("response", lambda r: problems.append(f"http {r.status}: {r.url}") if r.status >= 400 else None)
    51	        page.on(
    52	            "dialog", lambda d: d.accept() if d.type == "beforeunload" else d.dismiss()
    53	        )  # leave-page prompt on reload
    54	        page.goto(BASE + "/", wait_until="networkidle")
    55	        print("title:", page.title())
    56	        page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_home.png", full_page=True)
    57	
    58	        for sample, expect in EXPECTED.items():
    59	            page.click(f"[data-sample={sample}]")
    60	            page.wait_for_selector("#status.is-busy", timeout=15000)
    61	            page.wait_for_selector("#status:not(.is-busy)", timeout=60000)
    62	            page.wait_for_selector("#results:not([hidden]) .verdict", timeout=60000)
    63	            heading = page.inner_text(".verdict .usa-alert__heading")
    64	            timing = page.inner_text(".verdict .timing").strip()[:60]
    65	            rows = page.locator(".checklist tbody tr").count()
    66	            crops = page.locator(".checklist img.crop").count()
    67	            polys = page.locator(".overlay polygon").count()
    68	            print(f"[{sample}] {heading!r} | {timing} | rows={rows} crops={crops} polygons={polys}")
    69	            if expect and heading != expect:
    70	                problems.append(f"sample {sample}: expected {expect!r}, got {heading!r}")
    71	            page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_{sample}.png", full_page=True)
    72	            page.locator(".checklist tbody tr").first.click()
    73	            print("   active polygons after row click:", page.locator(".overlay polygon.is-active").count())
    74	
    75	        # Decision, note and export on the single screen (the same controls as the batch screen)
    76	        page.locator("#decision .decision-btns button", has_text="Approve").click()
    77	        if page.locator("#decision button[aria-pressed='true']", has_text="Approve").count() != 1:
    78	            problems.append("single: Approve did not stay pressed")
    79	        page.fill("#decision .note-input", "checked the heading on the image")
    80	        page.locator("#decision .note-input").press("Tab")
    81	        with page.expect_download() as dl:
    82	            page.locator("#decision .decision-actions button", has_text="Export").click()
    83	        with open(dl.value.path(), encoding="utf-8-sig") as f:
    84	            lines = f.read().splitlines()
    85	        if len(lines) != 2 or '"approve"' not in lines[1] or "checked the heading" not in lines[1]:
    86	            problems.append("single: export lacks the decision or the note")
    87	        print("single: decision recorded and exported")
    88	
    89	        # Accessibility page: the display choice applies at once, survives a reload, and restores.
    90	        page.click("a[data-view=accessibility]")
    91	        page.wait_for_selector("#view-accessibility:not([hidden])", timeout=5000)
    92	        page.click("label[for=theme-dark]")
    93	        if page.get_attribute("html", "data-theme") != "dark":
    94	            problems.append("theme: Dark did not apply")
    95	        page.reload(wait_until="networkidle")
    96	        if page.get_attribute("html", "data-theme") != "dark" or not page.is_checked("#theme-dark"):
    97	            problems.append("theme: Dark did not persist across a reload")
    98	        page.click("a[data-view=check]")
    99	        page.click("[data-sample=problem]")
   100	        page.wait_for_selector("#status:not(.is-busy)", timeout=60000)
   101	        page.wait_for_selector("#results:not([hidden]) .verdict", timeout=60000)
   102	        page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_dark.png", full_page=True)
   103	        page.click("a[data-view=accessibility]")
   104	        page.click("label[for=theme-light]")
   105	        if page.get_attribute("html", "data-theme") != "light":
   106	            problems.append("theme: Light did not restore")
   107	        print("theme: dark applied, persisted, restored")
   108	
   109	        mobile = browser.new_context(
   110	            viewport={"width": 390, "height": 844}, device_scale_factor=2, bypass_csp=True
   111	        ).new_page()
   112	        mobile.goto(BASE + "/", wait_until="networkidle")
   113	        mobile.click("[data-sample=clean]")
   114	        mobile.wait_for_selector("#results:not([hidden]) .verdict", timeout=60000)
   115	        mobile.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_mobile.png", full_page=True)
   116	        if mobile.evaluate("document.documentElement.scrollWidth > document.documentElement.clientWidth"):
   117	            problems.append("mobile: page scrolls horizontally")
   118	        browser.close()
   119	
   120	    print("\nPROBLEMS:" if problems else "\nno problems detected")
   121	    for x in problems[:40]:
   122	        print(" -", x[:300])
   123	    return 1 if problems else 0
   124	
   125	
   126	if __name__ == "__main__":
   127	    sys.exit(main())


===== FILE: README.md (current text at 3d9bdad, line-numbered) =====
     1	# Label Check
     2	
     3	**A prototype that reads alcohol beverage label artwork, compares it with what the COLA application
     4	says, checks the government warning word for word, and shows a compliance agent exactly where on the
     5	label each answer came from. In a few seconds. The tool recommends; the agent decides. Nothing is stored.**
     6	
     7	> Prototype for the Treasury take-home exercise. Not an official U.S. Government system.
     8	> Built and documented by Daniel R. Cooley, directing AI coding agents (see [Tools](#tools-and-how-this-was-built)).
     9	
    10	- **Live prototype:** https://labelcheck.dev (Azure Container Apps, always on)
    11	- **Approach in two pages:** [docs/APPROACH.md](docs/APPROACH.md)
    12	- **Where each requirement is met:** [docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md](docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md)
    13	
    14	![Home screen: two numbered steps, one button, three samples](docs/img/home.png)
    15	
    16	## Try it in 60 seconds
    17	
    18	1. Open the live prototype.
    19	2. Press **Clean artwork**. In about two to three seconds you get a checklist: every field matches,
    20	   the warning is exact, and each row shows the label pixels it was read from.
    21	3. Press **Problem label**. The alcohol content mismatches (45% claimed, 47.5% on the label) and the
    22	   warning heading is in title case. The brand differs only by letter case and is correctly treated
    23	   as a match with a note, which is Dave's "STONE'S THROW" example from the brief. Under the
    24	   result, record your decision (Approve, Reject or Flag, with a note), export it as CSV, or print it.
    25	4. Open **Batch** and press **Load a demo batch**. Five applications and ten images stream in;
    26	   filter to "Needs attention", record a decision, export the CSV.
    27	
    28	Then upload your own images. Anything the tool cannot read tells you why and what to do.
    29	
    30	![Result for the problem label: verdict, checklist with evidence crops, highlighted label](docs/img/result-problem-label.png)
    31	
    32	## The four things the brief's authors emphasized
    33	
    34	The interviews in the brief carry the real requirements, and four phrases are bolded in the original.
    35	Each one is met by design and checked by a test or a measurement, not by a claim.
    36	
    37	| Emphasized in the brief | What this prototype does | Evidence |
    38	|---|---|---|
    39	| "If we can't get results back in about **5 seconds**, nobody's going to use it." | OCR runs in-process on a small neural model; the images of one application are read in parallel; every result prints its own timing. | Local: front+back application median 2.5 s, p95 2.9 s on two workers. Deployed (Azure, 2 vCPU), measured by the server's own clock: one person, front+back, median 2.8 s, p95 3.0 s over 20 runs; a front label alone, which triggers the sideways re-read, median 3.6 s, p95 3.9 s; two people submitting at once, p95 4.3 s. Wall time from a laptop adds the network: within 0.1 s of those on a fast connection, 1.5 s more on a congested one (see [Measured](#measured)) |
    40	| "something **my mother could figure out**" | One screen, two numbered steps, one big button, three one-click samples, U.S. Web Design System, statuses as icon + word + color, keyboard and screen-reader friendly, pressed buttons marked in text as well as color so Windows contrast themes show them, and an Accessibility page with the accessibility statement and a Light / Dark / match-my-device display choice kept in the browser only. | Observed usability test: _USABILITY_RESULT_ |
    41	| "**handle batch uploads**" (200 to 300 at once) | Batch screen: a folder of images plus a spreadsheet; rows stream in; filters, decisions, notes, export; pairing by CSV column or filename prefix; leftovers assigned by hand. | 300 images (150 applications) through the real screen with zero errors: 275 s locally, 315 s on the deployed app from a laptop browser: [docs/LOADTEST.md](docs/LOADTEST.md) |
    42	| The warning "has to be **exact**. Like, word-for-word" | Every word and punctuation mark compared with 27 CFR 16.21 (text verified from the eCFR API), ignoring only letter case and spacing next to punctuation. Only an exact match passes. Punctuation and single-character differences are "Needs review" with a diff (usually OCR noise); a changed, added or missing word is a mismatch. | `tests/unit/test_warning.py` golden cases; the "Problem label" sample |
    43	
    44	And the constraint behind the architecture: Marcus's network "blocks outbound traffic to a lot of
    45	domains". The verification path makes **no network calls**. Models are in the repository, the page
    46	loads no CDN assets, and a test blocks every socket and runs a full verification
    47	(`tests/integration/test_no_egress.py`). You can run the container with networking disabled.
    48	
    49	## How it works
    50	
    51	```mermaid
    52	flowchart LR
    53	  A[Label images<br/>PNG JPEG GIF WebP TIFF BMP] --> B[Intake<br/>signature sniff, bomb guard,<br/>EXIF orientation, downscale]
    54	  B --> C[OCR pool<br/>PP-OCR on ONNX Runtime<br/>one engine per worker]
    55	  C --> D[Lines with boxes<br/>mapped back to the original image]
    56	  D --> E[Pure pipeline<br/>normalize, parse, match,<br/>warning comparator, verdict]
    57	  F[Application fields<br/>as written] --> E
    58	  E --> G[Checks + evidence + verdict]
    59	  G --> H[Browser<br/>checklist, crops, highlights,<br/>batch triage and export]
    60	```
    61	
    62	- **Matching has three outcomes.** Match (same after case, accents, quotes, spacing), Needs review
    63	  (close, usually OCR noise, look at the crop), Mismatch or Not found. Heuristic findings are never
    64	  failures; only clear text or numeric differences are: a brand the label does not carry, a number
    65	  that disagrees, an origin statement naming another country, a warning that is missing or worded
    66	  differently. A class/type worded differently from the application's description, a bottler line
    67	  that names another party, or a value the engine could not read are review items with the reason,
    68	  because none of those is a proven defect (decision D-041, reached against 146 real applications).
    69	- **Numbers are compared as numbers.** "45% Alc./Vol. (90 Proof)" in the application and
    70	  "45% ALC/VOL" on the label agree; proof is cross-checked against percent; "12 FL OZ (355 mL)"
    71	  agrees with "355 mL". Net contents may be left blank (the COLA form itself carries none); the
    72	  result then shows what the label says and asks for confirmation instead of a match.
    73	- **The warning check is literal.** The recognizer decodes with an English (printable ASCII) alphabet, so
    74	  there is one transcript, and every word and punctuation mark of it is compared with the regulation's text;
    75	  only letter case and the spacing next to punctuation are ignored. Bold type is measured from the pixels:
    76	  the heading's stroke weight against the rest of its line, a match when clearly heavier, a review item when
    77	  the same, and "not checked" with the reason when the print is too small to measure (D-044). The canonical text, the format rules
    78	  (capitals, bold, contrast, minimum type size) and the container-size lists are documented with their sources in
    79	  [docs/REGULATIONS.md](docs/REGULATIONS.md). Items the tool cannot assess from an image say
    80	  "Not checked" instead of pretending.
    81	- **Batch is orchestrated by the browser.** Each image is read exactly once (the sideways re-read of
    82	  the single-label screen is not run in a batch); each application is compared as soon as its images
    83	  are read; the server keeps no state, refuses rather than queues when it is full (HTTP 429 with
    84	  Retry-After), and gives a waiting person priority over a batch.
    85	
    86	## Run it
    87	
    88	**With Docker (recommended):**
    89	
    90	```bash
    91	docker build -t label-check .
    92	docker run --rm -p 8000:8000 --tmpfs /tmp --network none label-check
    93	# open http://localhost:8000
    94	```
    95	
    96	`--network none` is optional and proves the point: the app needs no outside services.
    97	
    98	**Without Docker (Python 3.12 or newer):**
    99	
   100	```bash
   101	python -m venv .venv
   102	.venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt          # Windows: .venv\Scripts\pip
   103	.venv/bin/pip install --no-deps -r requirements-ocr.txt   # rapidocr without its desktop-OpenCV dependency
   104	.venv/bin/uvicorn app.main:app --port 8000
   105	```
   106	
   107	For a few seconds after start the verification endpoints return 503 while the models load and the
   108	page shows "starting"; `GET /api/v1/ready` turns 200 when they are warm.
   109	
   110	**API (for integration, or for a script):**
   111	
   112	```bash
   113	curl -F 'application={"beverage_type":"spirits","brand_name":"OLD TOM DISTILLERY","class_type":"Kentucky Straight Bourbon Whiskey","alcohol_content":"45% Alc./Vol. (90 Proof)","net_contents":"750 mL"}' \
   114	     -F images=@app/static/samples/clean_front.png -F images=@app/static/samples/clean_back.png \
   115	     http://localhost:8000/api/v1/verify
   116	```
   117	
   118	Endpoints: `POST /api/v1/verify`, `POST /api/v1/extract`, `POST /api/v1/compare`,
   119	`POST /api/v1/csv/parse`, `GET /api/v1/csv/template`, `GET /api/v1/health`,
   120	`GET /api/v1/openapi.json`. Batch clients send `X-Batch: 1` and honor `Retry-After`.
   121	
   122	**Tests and tools:**
   123	
   124	```bash
   125	pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
   126	pytest -m "not integration"        # 168 fast unit tests, no OCR
   127	pytest                              # + 18 integration tests through the API with the real engine
   128	python tests/browser/smoke_single.py   # headless browser checks (needs a running server + Playwright)
   129	ruff check . && mypy                # lint and strict typing
   130	python tools/evaluate.py            # accuracy and latency table -> docs/EVAL.md
   131	python tools/loadtest.py --url http://localhost:8000 --mode burst --concurrency 16
   132	python tools/make_labels.py --out tests/fixtures/labels --seed 42 --degraded --problems
   133	```
   134	
   135	## Measured
   136	
   137	Numbers come from `tools/evaluate.py` and `tools/loadtest.py`; the files they write are committed.
   138	
   139	| What | Result | Where |
   140	|---|---|---|
   141	| Field match rate on clean artwork (recall) | 100% on all six fields (60 of 60 checks); warning exact on 10 of 10 | [docs/EVAL.md](docs/EVAL.md) |
   142	| False-alarm rate on clean artwork | 0.0% (0 of 60 field checks) | [docs/EVAL.md](docs/EVAL.md) |
   143	| Degraded images (rotation, blur, glare, low contrast, perspective, small, JPEG, sideways) | fields 95% to 100%; warning exact on 18 of 20; 17 of 20 cases ready, 2 need review, 1 issue (a label shrunk to a third of its size); both sideways photographs ready after the rotation retry, at about 6.8 s | [docs/EVAL.md](docs/EVAL.md) |
   144	| Planted defects detected | 5 of the 5 the tool assesses (wrong ABV, title-case heading, altered wording, missing statement, and the statement set all in bold, caught by measuring stroke weight); the remaining planted defect (tiny type, a physical size) is not assessed and is reported as such | [docs/EVAL.md](docs/EVAL.md) |
   145	| Per-application latency, local (two images, 2 workers) | median 2,536 ms, p95 3,059 ms on clean artwork | [docs/EVAL.md](docs/EVAL.md) |
   146	| Real approved labels: 150 applications from TTB's Public COLA Registry, 50 each spirits, wine, malt, artwork as submitted | Warning statement located on 99% (two misses: one upload set with no statement on it, one label that prints it vertically in type too small to read at the registry's size); wording exact on 62% of those located, small-print slips flagged for review on 22%, wording issues on 16% (including three approved labels that genuinely deviate); the heading measured clearly heavier than the body on 43% and the print was too small to measure on the rest, with no approved label flagged; brand name matched or sent to review on 88%; the registered applicant line (names and address as COLAs stores them) matched the label's bottler line on 70%, the rest sent to review with the reason; country of origin found on 92% of imports; alcohol statement read on 81%, net contents on 80% | [docs/EVAL_REAL.md](docs/EVAL_REAL.md) |
   147	| 146 real applications through the deployed batch screen, with a spreadsheet of the registry's own fields (names and address as registered, class code description, no alcohol or net contents because the COLA form carries neither) | 105 need review, 41 issues (decorative brand type the engine could not read, and warning statements read with wording differences), 0 errors; 0 ready, because a blank net contents always asks for confirmation; the registered bottler line matched the label on 102 of 146. Before D-041 the same run gave 144 issues, nearly all from the bottler line and the class description | [docs/DECISIONS.md](docs/DECISIONS.md) D-040, D-041; [docs/reviews/006-registry-fields.dispositions.md](docs/reviews/006-registry-fields.dispositions.md) |

Wall time from this laptop matched those within 0.1 s in the morning (median 2,674 ms one client) and ran about 1.5 s above them on a congested afternoon connection; both runs are in the log | [docs/LOADTEST.md](docs/LOADTEST.md) |

   153	| Inside the CI container (GitHub runner, 1 worker, networking disabled) | ready 2.1 s after start; front+back application verified in 3.1 s | CI run 33821661824 |
   154	
   155	**Error analysis, and one decision worth reading.** The first evaluation showed the multilingual recognizer occasionally emitting an accented letter for a plain one on clean artwork ("alcoholič", "drivé"), which turned an exact warning into "Needs review" on 2 of 10 labels. An early build stripped accents before the comparison; the independent reviewer rejected that, correctly, because a legal "exact" must not paper over instrument error. I then tested a native-resolution re-read of the warning lines, which did not converge (one label fixed, a different accent on another, and a previously exact label lost a letter). The fix that shipped is a recognizer configuration, not a comparison shortcut: the decoder's alphabet is restricted to printable ASCII for every line, the same choice as using an English recognizer and with none of the latency. There is one transcript and it is compared literally. The consequence, stated in the limits: genuinely accented print is read as its base letter, which the field comparisons already tolerate and the evidence crop shows; and the direction that matters legally, an accented character printed inside the warning would be read as its base letter and could pass as exact, is stated there too. The setting is a documented switch (`TTB_OCR_ASCII_ALPHABET`). The remaining degraded misses are a label shrunk to a third of its size (one issue, one review) and one tilted photograph sent to review.
   156	
   157	**What 150 real labels changed.** The synthetic corpus has exact ground truth and no surprises, so
   158	on the second day I fetched 150 approved applications from TTB's Public COLA Registry (50 each of
   159	spirits, wine and malt; `tools/cola_fetch.py`, polite and rate-limited) and ran the pipeline on
   160	their label images as submitted (`tools/evaluate_real.py`, [docs/EVAL_REAL.md](docs/EVAL_REAL.md);
   161	the artwork stays local because it belongs to the brand owners). The first pass was humbling: the
   162	warning was never judged exact, because most approved labels print the whole statement in
   163	capitals and the comparator treated letter case as wording; small labels print the statement
   164	vertically along one edge and the upright read never saw it; applicants register "Green Cheek Beer
   165	Company" where the label prints "BREWED BY GREEN CHEEK BEER CO."; and a column filter in the
   166	warning finder silently truncated any statement read from a rotated image, a bug the sideways tier
   167	of the synthetic corpus had been reporting as two "issues" all along. Each of those became a
   168	decision (D-035 to D-038), a code change and a test. The regulation supports the case change:
   169	16.22 requires capitals only for the two anchor words. Hand-checking records against their images
   170	also found the other kind of result: three approved tequila labels that genuinely print "WOMAN" and
   171	"BECAUSE OF RISK OF BIRTH DEFECTS", which the tool reports as a wording issue with the exact words
   172	in the diff. That is the tool working as Jenny asked.
   173	
   174	**And what that round cost, caught the next morning.** The sideways re-read shipped as "read every
   175	image again, turned both ways, then at full resolution, whenever no statement was found". Measured
   176	on the deployed build the next morning, that was a mistake in two places: the batch screen reads
   177	each image before it knows its application, so every front label (no statement by design) paid
   178	three extra reads and the 300-image batch went from 364 s to 841 s; and a single front label on the
   179	single-label screen took 8.6 s against a five-second requirement. The reviewer's fifth pass found the
   180	same thing from the code, plus two real defects in the same change: exactness had been made to
   181	ignore spacing by deleting every space, so "womens hould" would have passed, and folding corporate
   182	forms away could pass "LLC" against "Inc.". The build described here is the corrected one
   183	([D-039](docs/DECISIONS.md)): the re-read is one round of parallel reads, at most one per worker,
   184	on the single-label screen only; the batch screen reads every image exactly once; exactness
   185	compares the words and punctuation marks; a corporate-form mismatch is a review item. Every
   186	number in this README was re-measured after that change.
   187	
   188	Engine selection and thread scaling are in [docs/BAKEOFF.md](docs/BAKEOFF.md) and
   189	[docs/OCR_EVAL.md](docs/OCR_EVAL.md). Enforced limits and known weaknesses, stated plainly, are in
   190	[docs/LIMITS.md](docs/LIMITS.md).
   191	
   192	## For reviewers with a scoring sheet
   193	
   194	| Criterion | Where to look |
   195	|---|---|
   196	| Correctness and completeness of core requirements | The three samples; [docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md](docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md); `tests/integration/test_verify_api.py` |
   197	| Code quality and organization | `app/pipeline/` (pure functions, unit tested), `app/ocr/pool.py` (admission policy with tests), `app/static/*.js` (no build step, readable in one sitting); ruff + mypy strict in CI |
   198	| Appropriate technical choices for the scope | [docs/APPROACH.md](docs/APPROACH.md), [docs/DECISIONS.md](docs/DECISIONS.md) (including where the first plan was wrong), [docs/OCR_EVAL.md](docs/OCR_EVAL.md) |
   199	| User experience and error handling | Upload a PDF, a 50 MB image, an empty file, or a sideways photo; try the page at 200% zoom, keyboard only, or on a phone |
   200	| Attention to requirements | [docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md](docs/REQUIREMENTS_TRACE.md), one row per stakeholder statement |
   201	| Creative problem-solving | Evidence crops next to every value; noise-versus-wording classification for the warning; browser-orchestrated batch that keeps the server stateless; measured admission control; the no-egress test; the label generator with exact ground truth |
   202	
   203	## Security and data handling
   204	
   205	No storage, no logging of label content, no outside calls, signature-based file checks, size and
   206	pixel guards, a strict Content-Security-Policy, a non-root container, per-client and global capacity
   207	limits with honest 429s. No sign-in, deliberately: a fake login proves nothing, and the production
   208	path (Entra ID with PIV in front of the ingress, an audit record with a retention policy) is
   209	described in [docs/SECURITY.md](docs/SECURITY.md).
   210	
   211	## Tools, and how this was built
   212	
   213	Python 3.12, FastAPI, Pydantic, NumPy, Pillow, RapidFuzz, RapidOCR with PP-OCR models on ONNX
   214	Runtime (vendored, hash-pinned), U.S. Web Design System 3.14 (vendored), pytest, ruff, mypy,
   215	Playwright, Docker, Azure Container Apps. Third-party licenses: [THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md).
   216	
   217	This was built by directing AI coding agents: Claude Code wrote code under my direction and Codex
   218	served as an independent reviewer. The requirements analysis, the architecture, the review
   219	passes, and every entry in [docs/DECISIONS.md](docs/DECISIONS.md) are mine, including the places
   220	where the agent's first proposal was wrong and how it was caught. The design review and its
   221	point-by-point dispositions are in [docs/reviews/](docs/reviews/). Conventions for the agents are in
   222	[AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md). Commits carry the agent trailer.
   223

   228	## Configuration
   229	
   230	| Variable | Default | Meaning |
   231	|---|---|---|
   232	| `TTB_OCR_WORKERS` | CPU count (max 8) | OCR engines, one per worker thread; also the capacity limit |
   233	| `TTB_OCR_MAX_SIDE` | 1280 | Images are downscaled to this longest side before OCR |
   234	| `TTB_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` | 10 MB | Per-image cap |
   235	| `TTB_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` | 40 MB | Per-request cap |
   236	| `TTB_MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS` | 25,000,000 | Decompression-bomb guard |
   237	| `TTB_MAX_IMAGES_PER_APPLICATION` | 6 | Images per verify call |
   238	| `TTB_PER_CLIENT_INFLIGHT` | 4 | Concurrent requests per client |
   239	| `TTB_INTERACTIVE_WAIT_SECONDS` | 8 | How long an interactive request waits for a worker |
   240	| `TTB_WARNING_RESCUE` | true | Single-label screen only: when no usable warning is found upright, one more round of reads in parallel, at most one per worker (the images turned sideways, since small labels print it vertically; large artwork once at full resolution, up to 2048 px). Batch requests read every image exactly once |
   241	| `TTB_MATCH_REVIEW_THRESHOLD` | 80 | Fuzzy score at or above which a non-exact text match is Needs review rather than a mismatch |
   242	| `TTB_TRUST_PROXY` | false | Take the client address from `X-Forwarded-For` (set true behind a platform proxy) |
   243	| `TTB_AGENCY_NAME` | unset | Optional agency name for internal branding (no seals in the public prototype) |
   244	| `GIT_SHA` | dev | Shown in the footer and in `/api/v1/health` |
   245	
   246	## Repository layout
   247	
   248	```
   249	app/            FastAPI application: routes/, ocr/ (engine + pool), pipeline/ (pure logic), static/ (UI), models/ (vendored OCR models)
   250	tests/          unit/ (fast), integration/ (real engine through the API), fixtures/labels (46 committed images + manifest)
   251	tools/          make_labels.py, evaluate.py, loadtest.py, vendor_models.py, ocr_eval2.py
   252	docs/           APPROACH, REQUIREMENTS_TRACE, LIMITS, SECURITY, REGULATIONS (+ regs/ XML), DECISIONS, EVAL, LOADTEST, OCR_EVAL, BAKEOFF, reviews/
   253	Dockerfile      python:3.12-slim, non-root, health check
   254	```

     1	# Decision log
     2	
     3	Dated, in the order they were made. Each entry says what was decided, why, and what evidence or
     4	review changed it. Decisions marked **(corrected)** record a first proposal that was wrong and
     5	how it was caught. Owner of every decision: Daniel R. Cooley. Builder: Claude Code. Reviewer: Codex.
     6	
     7	| ID | Date | Decision | Why | Evidence / source |
     8	|---|---|---|---|---|
     9	| D-001 | 2026-09-03 | Python + FastAPI backend, vanilla HTML/JS frontend styled with the U.S. Web Design System, one container. | Primary language of the author; strongest OCR ecosystem; one URL; no build step; no CDN so it works behind a restrictive firewall; USWDS is the federal standard and Section 508 ready. | Brief: Marcus on firewall; Sarah on simplicity. |
    10	| D-002 | 2026-09-03 | OCR runs in-process. No LLM or cloud vision API in the core verification path. Pluggable provider interface for an optional, off-by-default second opinion. | The brief's firewall note and 5-second limit rule cloud calls out; determinism and pixel-traceable findings matter for a government audit tool. | Brief: Marcus, Sarah. Review 001 agreed. |
    11	| D-003 | 2026-09-03 | The tool recommends; the agent decides. No auto-approve or auto-reject anywhere. | Dave's "you need judgment"; federal AI guidance requires human oversight for rights-impacting decisions. | Brief: Dave. |
    12	| D-004 | 2026-09-03 | No Treasury or TTB seals. Prototype banner instead. Configurable agency branding slot left empty in the public demo. | Federal law restricts use of Treasury symbols in ways implying endorsement; the reviewers' own seal in the invitation email is a signature graphic, not a project asset. | Discussion with author. |
    13	| D-005 | 2026-09-03 | RapidOCR (ONNX Runtime) is the sole engine; Tesseract dropped. Final configuration: bundled PP-OCRv6 small detector and recognizer, angle classifier off, detector side 640, input 1280 px, recognizer batch 16, one intra-op thread per engine. Models vendored with SHA-256. | Measured on 46 synthetic images: RapidOCR recovered ABV 90-100% and net contents 90-100% across degradations; Tesseract was 3x faster but lost small text (ABV 76%, net 71%) and failed on sideways images. Among RapidOCR configurations, the bundled small models matched or beat the v4 and English models on accuracy at 40-50% less single-thread time, and they need no download at all. | docs/BAKEOFF.md, docs/OCR_EVAL.md (runs A-H). |
    14	| D-006 | 2026-09-03 | Preprocessing is minimal: EXIF transpose, downscale, 90-degree retry on low confidence. **(corrected)** | The plan had deskew, CLAHE, glare suppression and zoom passes. Measurement showed the engine already handles glare, blur, perspective, low contrast and small text; only sideways images failed. | docs/BAKEOFF.md; Review 001 3.2. |
    15	| D-007 | 2026-09-03 | Pin Python 3.12 in the container and CI; build the lock on linux/amd64; vendor model files with SHA-256; no downloads at build or run. **(corrected)** | On Python 3.13 pip silently resolved the OCR library to a 2023 release whose thread settings were ignored, which produced misleading concurrency numbers. | Review 001 4.2 and 4.3; perf run. |
    16	| D-008 | 2026-09-03 | A COLA application is a set of images (front, back, neck), merged before checks. **(corrected)** | First plan assumed one image per application; the warning is usually on the back label, so that design would flag every front label as missing the warning. | Author review pass 2. |
    17	| D-009 | 2026-09-03 | Primary input is label artwork; photographs are a best-effort secondary tier reported separately. **(corrected)** | Brief says agents look at "label artwork"; Jenny calls photos "maybe out of scope". | Author review pass 3. |
    18	| D-010 | 2026-09-03 | Application fields are accepted as strings exactly as written (e.g. "45% Alc./Vol. (90 Proof)"); the same parser runs on both sides; comparison is numeric. **(corrected)** | The brief's sample shows string fields; a bare numeric field would have failed the sample. | Author review pass 3. |
    19	| D-011 | 2026-09-03 | Warning check: exact match is the only Pass. Near matches are Needs review with a character diff. Anchor caps and bold, body not bold, contrast and size are Needs review findings with evidence. | 27 CFR 16.21 text and 16.22 format verified from the eCFR API; Jenny: "It has to be exact." Review 001 1.4 tightened an earlier "OCR-tolerant" design. | docs/REGULATIONS.md; Review 001. |
    20	| D-012 | 2026-09-03 | Heuristic findings never produce a failure; only clear text or numeric mismatches do. | Keeps the false-alarm rate low (Dave's phone-system story) and leaves judgment with the agent. | Author review pass 5. |
    21	| D-013 | 2026-09-03 | Bottler name/address and country of origin are core comparisons when supplied; origin is required when the application is marked imported. **(corrected)** | The brief lists both as required label elements; the plan had demoted them to optional. | Review 001 1.6, 6.7. |
    22	| D-014 | 2026-09-03 | Batch pairing defaults to explicit filenames or a CSV column; fuzzy pairing is a confirmable suggestion, never automatic. **(corrected)** | Back labels often carry no brand, ABV or net contents, so automatic pairing could attach them to the wrong application with confidence. | Review 001 2.2. |
    23	| D-015 | 2026-09-03 | One deployment host (Azure Container Apps) plus a tested local Docker command; a second host is an emergency plan, not a parallel deployment. **(corrected)** | Two hosts doubled operational surface for a one-week prototype; Azure domains also have better odds of being reachable from a government network. | Review 001 2.1. |
    24	| D-016 | 2026-09-03 | One priority-aware capacity limiter; cooperative pass budgets instead of pretend timeouts; streaming size caps; tmpfs for temporary files. **(corrected)** | Two semaphores could exceed CPU; a deadline cannot stop a running inference; multipart can spool to disk. | Review 001 2.4, 2.5, 2.8. |
    25	| D-017 | 2026-09-03 | Standards of fill checked against the application's stated net contents only, Needs review only. | Real agents check container sizes; using application data removes OCR risk; the lists changed in January 2025 so a hard failure would be wrong. | docs/REGULATIONS.md; Review 001 3.4 (narrowed rather than cut). |
    26	| D-018 | 2026-09-03 | Acceptance gate for speed: deployed, end-to-end, front+back application p95 under 5 seconds under concurrent batch load. | The authors bolded the 5-second sentence; a per-image number on a laptop is not the same claim. | Brief emphasis; Review 001 1.1. |
    27	| D-019 | 2026-09-03 | AI assistance is disclosed openly: commit trailers kept, Claude Code listed under tools used, this log records where the AI's first proposal was wrong. | The role is AI-directed engineering and the panel knows the workflow; the direction and correction are the work product. | Author. |
    28	| D-020 | 2026-09-03 | OCR workers are a thread pool, one engine per thread with one ONNX intra-op thread each, N = vCPUs. Process pool kept only as a crash-isolation option. **(corrected twice)** | First perf run showed no scaling, which turned out to be the old library ignoring thread settings. With real controls, 2 threads gave 1.84x and 4 gave 3.1x the single-worker throughput, and a process pool gave identical numbers (0.79 vs 0.79 img/s at 2 workers; 1.33 vs 1.31 at 4). Threads are simpler and share memory. | docs/OCR_EVAL.md runs D, T, P. |
    29	| D-021 | 2026-09-03 | Per-image single-core cost is about 2.4 s on a laptop core, so the 5-second application gate needs at least 2 real vCPUs and the two images of an application must run in parallel. Hosting is chosen for dedicated CPU and measured cost, not theme. | Measured (run D). First estimate of Azure Container Apps at ~$150/month assumed a fully busy container; the idle rate that applies most of a review window puts a 2 vCPU / 4 GiB always-on app nearer $45-60/month, inside the $200 first-month credit. Dedicated 2-vCPU machines on Fly or Hetzner cost $8-62/month. **(corrected)** cost estimate. | docs/OCR_EVAL.md; provider price lists (to be confirmed at signup). |
    30	| D-022 | 2026-09-03 | The warning report carries an explicit `assessment` (exact, case, noise, wording, absent) and the verdict keys off it: exact passes; case and noise are Needs review; wording and absent are issues. Similarity thresholds no longer decide. | Thresholds blurred the one distinction that matters to Jenny: OCR noise versus a changed word. Word-level classification is explainable in one sentence. | Evaluation run 1 on the synthetic corpus; `tests/unit/test_warning.py`. |
    31	| D-023 | 2026-09-03 | Accents in the OCR read of the warning are removed before the literal comparison; a single-character difference inside a word of five or more letters, and digit/letter confusables including `1`/`i`, count as noise. (Superseded: D-029 for accents; D-035 and D-039 for what exactness ignores.) | The recognizer (a multilingual model) emits "alcoholič" and "drivé" for plain letters; the regulation text has no accented letters, so an accent is recognition noise by definition. Real wording changes replace whole words. **(corrected)** after evaluation run 1 showed clean artwork failing the warning check. | `docs/EVAL.md` history; `tests/unit/test_warning.py`. |
    32	| D-024 | 2026-09-03 | Reading order groups lines into rows by vertical overlap (at least half the shorter line's height), then sorts left to right. | Height-bucket sorting scrambled labels with mixed text sizes or tight line spacing, which truncated the warning span. **(corrected twice)**: first version divided by each line's own height, second by fixed buckets. | `tests/unit/test_match.py::test_reading_order_uses_vertical_overlap_not_height_buckets`. |
    33	| D-025 | 2026-09-03 | Batch-mode requests (`X-Batch: 1`) read their images one at a time; interactive requests fan out in parallel. | A batch request is refused rather than queued when no slot is free, so a two-image batch request that already held one slot was being refused halfway through, which the load test exposed as a 429 storm. **(corrected)** | `docs/LOADTEST.md`; `tests/integration/test_batch_mode.py`. |
    34	| D-026 | 2026-09-03 | The browser batch client limits concurrent requests, not concurrent applications, sizes the pool from `/health`, and backs off with growing delays on 429. The server reserves nothing statically; batch may use every slot unless a person is waiting. | The first design (one reserved slot, item-level concurrency) starved batch on a two-worker host and still produced refusals. Measured in the browser smoke test. **(corrected)** | `app/static/batch.js`; `tests/unit/test_pool.py`. |
    35	| D-027 | 2026-09-03 | Host: Azure Container Apps (2 vCPU / 4 GiB, minReplicas 1), account created on deploy day so the 30-day credit covers the review window; a neutral custom domain fronts it so the submitted URL survives a later move to another host. | Their cloud and the best odds of being reachable from a government network, at no cost for the window that matters; the domain removes the risk of a dead link if hosting changes. | Author decision after the cost correction. |
    36	| D-028 | 2026-09-04 | CI's container job must run its check with stdin attached and fail when the app is not ready; warm-up failures are surfaced in health and readiness. Shell scripts on this machine lose backslashes, so every multi-line edit goes through a script file. **(corrected)** | The first container job passed while running nothing (no `-i` on `docker exec`) and the app never became ready because system libraries were missing; an origin regex had literal backspace bytes from the same shell behavior. Both were caught by the Codex code review and by reading the raw job log. | Review 002; CI runs 33818820324, 33819136086. |
    37	| D-029 | 2026-09-04 | Exact means exact: accents in the OCR read of the warning are noise (Needs review), not stripped before the literal comparison. **(corrected)** (Refined by D-035 and D-039: exact ignores letter case and spacing next to punctuation, nothing else.) | I had stripped accents to keep clean artwork at "exact"; the reviewer was right that a legal status must not paper over an instrument's uncertainty. The evaluation reports the resulting false alarms honestly. | Review 002 item 1.6. |
    38	| D-030 | 2026-09-04 | When the label images of one application state different alcohol contents, that contradiction is a mismatch in itself, regardless of which one agrees with the application. | A front label at 40% and a back label at 45% cannot both be right; preferring the one that matches the application would hide the defect. Percent statements now also need an alcohol-by-volume marker so "5% alcohol flavoring" is not parsed as a statement. | Review 002 item 1.5; integration test wrong_abv. |
    39	| D-031 | 2026-09-04 | A worker slot is held until the OCR thread finishes, even when the client disconnects; batch requests hold one slot for all their images. **(corrected)** | Releasing on cancellation oversubscribed the CPU; per-image admission could refuse a multi-image batch request halfway. Both pinned by unit tests. | Review 002 items 1.1, 1.2. |
    40	| D-032 | 2026-09-04 | The recognizer decodes with its alphabet restricted to printable ASCII, for every line, as a configuration of the OCR engine (`TTB_OCR_ASCII_ALPHABET`, default on). "Exact" keeps its meaning: one transcript, compared literally. **(author's decision, against the reviewer's ranking)** | The multilingual model emitted stray accented letters on clean artwork (2 of 10 labels), turning exact warnings into Needs review. Review 003 ranked "do nothing" first and warned against overriding an accent-capable read with a second opinion; it also proposed a native-resolution re-read, which I tested and which did not converge (a different accent appeared, and a previously exact label lost a letter). Restricting the alphabet is not a second opinion: US labels are English text and the regulation is pure ASCII, so it is the same choice as using an English recognizer, with none of the latency. Genuinely accented print is read as its base letter; comparisons already fold accents and the evidence crop shows the print. | docs/reviews/003-accents.*; experiment in the decision record; evaluation re-run in docs/EVAL.md. |
    41	| D-033 | 2026-09-04 | Large JPEGs are decoded at reduced size (libjpeg scaling by 2, 4 or 8, never below the working size); the canonical size and the box scale come from the file header and its EXIF orientation, never from the decoded image. **(corrected)** | The reduced-size decode added after review 002 recorded the decoded size as the image size, so boxes and evidence crops would have been in the wrong coordinate space whenever it engaged. It was dormant only because its 2x margin could not engage below the 25 MP limit; lowering `TTB_OCR_MAX_SIDE` would have exposed it. Found in the late-session self-review; pinned by two unit tests (plain and EXIF-rotated). The decode now engages for 12 MP phone photos, which cost a quarter of the memory and time. | tests/unit/test_images.py; docs/reviews/004-late-session.*. |
    42	| D-034 | 2026-09-04 | Ready requires every check to be complete: only the bottler and country-of-origin checks may be "not checked", and only because the application omitted them. A required value the tool cannot interpret is Needs review. The alphabet wrapper fails closed and renormalizes confidence; the image no longer asserts proxy trust; nothing is published unless the test job passed. **(corrected)** | Review 004 found that an uninterpretable net-contents value could ride through to Ready, that the ASCII wrapper would silently fall back to the full alphabet on a shape change, that the container image trusted X-Forwarded-For wherever it ran, and that the container job could publish while tests failed. The alcohol comparison also treated a proof on its own line as a competing percent. All fixed with tests; the alphabet restriction itself (D-032) stays, with its false-pass direction now stated in the limits. | docs/reviews/004-late-session.*; tests/unit/test_parsers.py, test_alphabet.py, test_security.py. |
    43	| D-035 | 2026-09-04 | Three changes from the first real-label sample (150 approved COLAs from TTB's Public COLA Registry, `docs/EVAL_REAL.md`): (a) "exact" for the warning ignores letter case and spacing and nothing else, because 16.22 regulates capitals only for the two anchor words and most approved labels print the whole statement in capitals; the anchor's capitals stay a separate format check; (b) when no usable warning is found upright, the images are re-read sideways and only a warning span found that way is kept, because small labels print the statement vertically along one edge of an otherwise upright label (how many re-reads, when, and for which requests is D-039); (c) the bottler comparison folds "Bottled by" style prefixes and corporate forms (Company/Co., LLC, Inc.) on both sides, and the fuzzy review threshold drops from 90 to 80, because real labels abbreviate and decorative fonts read at 80-89 while the genuinely different names in the sample scored under 70. **(corrected)** | On the first 39 real records the warning was never "exact": 15 were all-capitals bodies and most of the rest were capitals plus one OCR slip; six "absent" warnings included a 50 mL label with the statement printed vertically; applicant names matched 0 of 39 because the registry spells out "Green Cheek Beer Company" where the label prints "BREWED BY GREEN CHEEK BEER CO."; two brand misses were decorative fonts scoring 86 and 89. The synthetic corpus, being generated, had none of these and would never have shown them. Each change is pinned by a unit or integration test; both evaluations were re-run. | docs/EVAL_REAL.md; tests/unit/test_warning.py, tests/unit/test_parsers.py; tests/integration/test_verify_api.py (sideways warning). |
    44	| D-036 | 2026-09-04 | The warning finder's "same column" filter measures overlap across the text direction, so lines read from a rotated image (vertical strips once mapped back) are joined into one statement. **(corrected)** | The filter compared horizontal overlap in the original image's coordinates; for a sideways photo, or a label printing the statement vertically, every line after the anchor was rejected as "another column" and the statement came back as a one-line fragment. This is why the sideways tier of the synthetic evaluation reported two issues, and it made the new sideways re-read useless until found by its test. Pinned by a unit test with vertical boxes, a unit test of the re-read with a fake engine, and an integration test with a sideways back label. | tests/unit/test_warning.py; tests/unit/test_services.py; tests/integration/test_verify_api.py. |
    45	| D-037 | 2026-09-04 | From hand-checking real records against their images: (a) the anchor may be split over two lines ("GOVERNMENT" set large, "WARNING:" beneath it); (b) large artwork whose statement is unreadable at the working size is read once more at bounded full resolution, only when no usable statement was found; (c) the noise classifier tolerates one slip (dropped, added, changed or two swapped letters) in words of four or more letters, hyphenated line breaks in capitals, and a bare number swept in from a neighbouring line, all still Needs review with the diff. A dropped or replaced word stays a wording issue. **(corrected)** | An absinthe label printed the anchor on two lines and was reported "absent"; a round 3 L label printed the statement in small type on a 2,930-pixel image and read as fragments; approved labels read as "Suregon", "YOURS", "CONSUMP- TION" and "26 (2)" were small print, not wording. One approved tequila label genuinely prints "WOMAN" and "OF RISK OF BIRTH DEFECTS": the tool now reports the first as review and the missing word as an issue, which is the right answer under the word-for-word rule. Both evaluations re-run. | docs/EVAL_REAL.md hand-checked cases; tests/unit/test_warning.py, tests/unit/test_services.py, tests/unit/test_normalize.py. |
    46	| D-038 | 2026-09-04 | Within a reading-order row, lines whose left edges sit within half a line's thickness of each other share a column and stay top to bottom; only genuinely separate columns are ordered left to right. **(corrected)** | Tight body text in a narrow column produces OCR boxes that overlap vertically, so consecutive statement lines were grouped as one "row" and sorted by their left edge; three pixels of edge jitter swapped two lines, and the joined statement read as "wording" with the same words deleted and re-inserted. Eight of the 42 wording verdicts on the real sample were this. Pinned by a unit test; both evaluations re-run. | tests/unit/test_warning.py (tight column); docs/EVAL_REAL.md. |
    47	| D-039 | 2026-09-04 | From review 005 and the measurements of the deployed real-label build: (a) the warning re-read runs for interactive requests only, as one extra round of reads in parallel across the workers, at most one read per worker: the images turned 270 then 90 degrees (270 unrolls a statement printed up the right edge, which three of the four such labels in the real sample do) and, for large artwork, once at full resolution (that read first when the heading was seen but the body was not); the orientations the rotation retry already read are used first and cost nothing; batch requests read every image exactly once. (b) "Exact" for the warning compares the sequence of words and punctuation marks, ignoring letter case and spacing next to punctuation, so a word boundary moved between letters ("womens hould") can never pass. (c) A bottler match whose corporate forms differ on the two sides ("LLC" against "Inc.") is Needs review. (d) In the statement finder a vertical strip and a horizontal line are never one column, and a rescued statement replaces what the kept read had in the same place. **(corrected)** | Measured on the deployed build: the batch screen reads each image before it knows its application, so every front label (no statement by design) paid three extra reads and single-image throughput fell from 0.98 to 0.23 images per second; a front-only upload took 8.6 s against a five-second requirement (`docs/LOADTEST.md`, af9e239 rows). The reviewer showed that deleting every space let "womens hould" pass as exact, that folding "LLC" and "Inc." away could pass two different legal entities, and that the re-read's outcome depended on server load. The fake-engine tests for the new round exposed (d): the strips of a sideways statement crossing an upright label's brand line were spliced together. A bound on reads was preferred to a clock budget because a clock would make the verdict depend on the machine's speed. | `docs/reviews/005-real-labels.*`; `docs/LOADTEST.md`; `tests/unit/test_services.py`, `test_warning.py`, `test_parsers.py`. |
    48	| D-040 | 2026-09-04 | Net contents may be left blank in the application (form and spreadsheet). A blank is Needs review: the result shows the net contents read from the label with its crop and asks the agent to confirm it; it can never be Ready on its own. Alcohol content was already optional; brand, class/type and beverage type stay required. | Hands-on testing with the real registry corpus: TTB's COLA form (F 5100.31) carries no net contents field, so a spreadsheet exported from real applications had every row rejected ("net_contents: Field required") and the batch screen then showed the wrong message ("Add images first", fixed in the same change). The brief's sample application lists net contents, so the comparison stays exactly as it was whenever a value is given. | `tests/unit/test_parsers.py` (blank net contents), `tests/unit/test_csvio.py`; `docs/EVAL_REAL.md` (the form's fields). |
    49	| D-041 | 2026-09-04 | Which findings may fail an application, decided against real application data (consult 006): (a) the bottler check takes the registered line apart (trade name, legal name, the name marked as used on the label, city, state, ZIP), tries the whole line and each name folded, and uses the city and state as corroboration: name and address read is a match, name only is Needs review, a different name on the label's own "bottled by" line or nothing resembling the name is Needs review with the reason; (b) class/type is Match when the text agrees and otherwise Needs review, never an issue; (c) an alcohol statement that is required but was not read, and a country of origin that was not read, are Needs review; an origin statement naming a different country, and numeric disagreements in alcohol or net contents, stay issues; (d) brand and the warning are unchanged; (e) from the first hands-on look at the result panels: a registered name printed whole inside the label's bottler line, with its city and state, is a match rather than a "close match"; when nothing on the label resembles the class description no "closest text" is shown; and digits from a barcode read glued onto a word of the warning statement ('or' read as 'OR88 186"223932') are noise for review, not a wording issue. | 146 real applications with a spreadsheet built from the registry's own fields came back 144 "Issues found": the registered bottler line (two names and a street address) never matched a label that prints "BREWED BY GREEN CHEEK BEER CO." and a city (141 rows), and the registry's class descriptions ("TABLE WHITE WINE") never match the designation a label prints ("Pinot Grigio") (80 rows). Neither is a proven defect: the applicant and the lawful bottler may differ, the tool has no table of permitted designations, and a line the engine did not read is a heuristic miss. The rule in AGENTS.md is that heuristic findings are Needs review, never Fail, and only clear text or numeric differences fail; the reviewer's consult (docs/reviews/006) reached the same matrix. The blanket "not found is an issue" rule was the brief's synthetic sample talking. | `docs/reviews/006-registry-fields.*`; `tests/unit/test_parsers.py` (bottler four ways, class, alcohol, origin), `tests/unit/test_normalize.py` (registered line parsing); `docs/EVAL_REAL.md`. |
    50	| D-042 | 2026-09-04 | An Accessibility page of its own, reached from the main navigation, holding the accessibility statement (moved out of About) and a display choice: Light (the default and the standard look), Dark (our own palette over the U.S. Web Design System, which has none), or match the device. The choice is kept in the browser's local storage only, applied before the first paint, and the page says so; nothing about labels or applications is stored. The beverage-type tiles on the single screen share one height. | The engineer asked for it after testing in a Windows contrast theme: the target users are agents over fifty, and a dark display is the first accommodation many of them reach for. Light stays the default because the reviewers evaluate the design system's look and the dark palette is ours; the choice is one click away and persists. Uneven tiles were a visible rough edge on the first screen. | `app/static/theme.js`, `app/static/app.css` (dark block); `tests/browser/smoke_single.py`. |
    51	| D-043 | 2026-09-04 | The single-label screen records the agent's decision (Approve, Reject or Flag, with a note) under the result and exports it as one CSV row with the same columns as the batch export, or prints it; the two screens share the same controls and export code. Nothing is stored on the server; the decision lives in the export and the printout. | Hands-on testing: after a "Needs review" result on the single screen there was no way to say "I checked it, it is fine" and no way to take the result away, while the batch screen had both. The tool recommends and the agent decides, and one application deserves the same decision and record as three hundred. | `app/static/render.js` (decisionControls, exportRow, downloadCsv); `tests/browser/smoke_single.py`. |
    52	| D-044 | 2026-09-04 | Bold type of the warning statement is measured from the pixels. Each OCR line carries the thickness of its ink strokes relative to its type height (`app/pipeline/typeface.py`: Otsu ink, distance transform, four times the mean distance); a line that carries the heading is measured as heading and remainder separately, splitting the box in proportion to the characters. The check compares the heading with the rest of its own line (same size, same face, same box) or, when the heading stands alone, its stroke thickness in pixels with the other lines'. A ratio of 1.15 or more is a Match on both format rows; 1.05 or less is Needs review on both, because the measurement cannot tell a heading that is not bold from a statement that is all bold; between is inconclusive, and print under 24 pixels tall or with strokes under 3.8 pixels at the working size is Not checked with that reason. Never a failure. | The author asked why bold was "Not checked". Calibration: on the synthetic corpus a bold heading over a regular body measures 1.17 to 1.24, the planted all-bold defect 0.99, blur 1.12 to 1.13 (inconclusive) and shrunken labels are not measured; on the 150 real approved labels 34 statements were measurable, 29 read as a heavier heading, 5 were inconclusive, none was flagged, and 16 were too small (most registry artwork of small labels is). Comparing the heading with the other lines instead of its own line was wrong on two real labels (box heights differ), and a first size gate let shrunken print through; both were caught by looking at the labels. The estimator overstates strokes under about 3 pixels, evenly for heading and body, which is why the gate is on stroke pixels. | `docs/EVAL.md` (all-bold defect now assessed); `docs/EVAL_REAL.md` (type-weight rows); `tests/unit/test_typeface.py`, `tests/unit/test_warning.py`, `tests/integration/test_verify_api.py`. |


===== FILE: docs/LIMITS.md (current text at 3d9bdad, line-numbered) =====
     1	# Limits, measured behavior, and known weaknesses
     2	
     3	Reviewers will push on this prototype, and they should. This page says what happens when they do.
     4	
     5	## Enforced limits
     6	
     7	| Limit | Value | What happens past it |
     8	|---|---|---|
     9	| Image size | 10 MB per image | HTTP 413, message names the file and the limit |
    10	| Request size | 40 MB, `Content-Length` required | HTTP 413 or 411 before the body is read (six images fit only if together they stay under 40 MB) |
    11	| Image dimensions | 25 megapixels | HTTP 413 "image too large", checked from the header before decoding; large JPEGs decode at reduced size |
    12	| Images per application | 6 | HTTP 422 |
    13	| Image formats | PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, TIFF, BMP by file signature | HTTP 415 with a format-specific hint (PDF, SVG, HEIC named) |
    14	| Concurrent OCR jobs | one per CPU (`TTB_OCR_WORKERS`) | Interactive requests wait up to 8 s for a slot, then 429; batch requests get 429 immediately with `Retry-After` |
    15	| Concurrent requests per client | 4 | HTTP 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, decided before the body is read |
    16	| Concurrent metered requests, all clients | 24 | HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 2`, decided before the body is read |
    17	| Compare items per call | 100 | HTTP 422 |
    18	| CSV rows / size | 5,000 rows, 2 MB | Rows beyond the limit are ignored with a warning; larger files are refused |
    19	| Batch size in the browser | no hard cap; tested with hundreds of images | Table paginates at 50 rows and re-renders at most a few times per second; result detail (crops, highlights) is rendered only when a row is expanded and cached |
    20	
    21	Health (`/api/v1/health`) never goes through the limiter, so it answers during a flood.
    22	Readiness (`/api/v1/ready`) returns 503 until the OCR engines are warm, so a platform never routes traffic to a cold instance.
    23	
    24	## Measured behavior
    25	
    26	- Accuracy and false-alarm rate by tier: `docs/EVAL.md` (generated by `tools/evaluate.py`).
    27	- Real approved labels from TTB's Public COLA Registry, 150 records: `docs/EVAL_REAL.md` (generated
    28	  by `tools/evaluate_real.py` on a sample fetched by `tools/cola_fetch.py`; the images stay local).
    29	- Throughput, latency percentiles and the 429 path under load: `docs/LOADTEST.md` (generated by
    30	  `tools/loadtest.py`, blocks name the host they ran against).
    31	- Engine selection and thread scaling: `docs/OCR_EVAL.md` and `docs/BAKEOFF.md`.
    32	- Deployed numbers are in the README under "Measured".
    33	
    34	## Known weaknesses, stated plainly
    35	
    36	1. **Bold type is measured, not read.** 27 CFR 16.22 requires "GOVERNMENT WARNING" in bold and the
    37	   rest not in bold. The tool measures the thickness of the ink strokes in the heading and in the
    38	   body of the statement, relative to the type height (`app/pipeline/typeface.py`, decision D-044).
    39	   A heading clearly heavier than the body is a Match on both counts; heading and body of the same
    40	   weight is Needs review on both, because the measurement cannot tell a heading that is not bold
    41	   from a statement that is all bold; a small difference is inconclusive. Print under about 24
    42	   pixels tall or with strokes under 3.5 pixels at the working size is "Not checked" with that
    43	   reason, which covers most registry artwork of small labels; the synthetic corpus and large
    44	   artwork measure well. Never a failure: it is a measurement of print, and the crop is shown.
    45	2. **Physical type size and characters per inch are not assessed.** They need a known scale.
    46	   Artwork files that carry DPI metadata would allow it; this build does not read DPI.
    47	3. **Photographs are best effort.** The primary input is label artwork as submitted to COLA.
    48	   The engine did well on the degraded test images, and sideways images are recovered by a
    49	   rotation retry, but glare across text or heavy blur will produce "unreadable" with a reason
    50	   rather than a guess.
    51	4. **Arched, curved or heavily stylized brand text** reads less reliably than straight text and
    52	   will land in "Needs review" more often. Seen on real labels: "Monk's Road" in blackletter read as
    53	   other words, "GREAT NOTION" read as "GreatNation". A fuzzy score of 80 to 89 against the nearest
    54	   text is Needs review with the crop; below that it is a mismatch or not found. The evidence crop
    55	   makes that a two-second decision.
    56	5. **The tool compares; it does not validate designations.** Whether "Kentucky Straight Bourbon
    57	   Whiskey" is a permissible class/type for this product, age statements, name-and-address
    58	   phrasing rules, alcohol-statement wording rules, laboratory tolerances and same-field-of-vision
    59	   placement are out of scope.
    60	6. **Standards of fill are advisory.** The container-size lists changed in January 2025 and stock
    61	   can be grandfathered, so an off-list size is "Needs review", never a failure. The check uses the
    62	   application's stated net contents, not the OCR reading.
    63	7. **English only.** The recognizer is decoded with a printable-ASCII alphabet (item 11) and is not
    64	   tuned for other scripts. Country-of-origin phrasing is compared, not validated against customs rules.
    65	8. **No persistence, by design.** Refreshing the batch page clears the session. The page says so
    66	   and the export is one click.
    67	9. **One container.** Throughput scales with CPUs and with replicas (the service is stateless), but
    68	   this prototype runs one instance. A 1,000-image batch at about two seconds per image on two
    69	   CPUs takes roughly ten to fifteen minutes and streams results the whole time.
    70	10. **Synthetic test corpus.** Ground truth is exact because the labels are rendered, and that also
    71	    means real-world variety is under-represented. Numbers in `docs/EVAL.md` are an upper bound
    72	    for artwork and a rough guide for photographs; `docs/EVAL_REAL.md` shows what 150 real approved
    73	    labels did to the same pipeline, and what changed because of them.
    74	11. **The recognizer's alphabet is restricted to printable ASCII.** The multilingual model occasionally
    75	    emitted stray accented letters on clean English artwork, so decoding is constrained to the characters
    76	    English label text can contain (`TTB_OCR_ASCII_ALPHABET`, on by default). A genuinely accented letter
    77	    on a label, such as a French back label or a brand like Château, is read as the best remaining class:
    78	    usually its base letter, occasionally nothing (the character is dropped); field comparisons already
    79	    ignore accents and the evidence crop shows the print. The direction to know about: an accented
    80	    character printed inside the warning statement would be read as its base letter and could pass as
    81	    exact, so that particular typographic defect is not caught. Confidence values are renormalized over
    82	    the allowed classes. Turn the flag off to read accented text literally, at the cost of the
    83	    stray-accent false alarms measured in `docs/EVAL.md`.
    84	12. **Fuzzy pairing is not attempted.** Images that are not listed in the CSV or named with the
    85	    application reference are shown as unmatched for the agent to assign. Guessing which back label
    86	    belongs to which application would be confidently wrong too often.
    87	13. **Statements printed sideways or in small type on large artwork.** Small labels often run the
    88	    government warning vertically along one edge of an otherwise upright label; large artwork
    89	    sometimes carries it in type that is unreadable once the image is scaled to the working size.
    90	    On the single-label screen, when no usable statement is found upright, the tool runs one more
    91	    round of reads in parallel, at most one per worker: the images turned 270 then 90 degrees (270
    92	    unrolls a statement printed up the right edge, the layout of three of the four such labels in
    93	    the real sample) and, for large artwork, once at up to 2,048 pixels (`TTB_WARNING_RESCUE`, on by
    94	    default). The round costs one read-time (about 2 s on the deployed sizing), so an upload with no
    95	    statement at all, such as a front label on its own, takes about twice as long to say so. What
    96	    the round can try scales with the workers: two workers try both turns of one image, or the
    97	    first turn of each image of a pair; the full-resolution read goes first only when the heading
    98	    was seen and the body was not.
    99	    A statement the round does not reach is reported absent. The batch screen reads every image
   100	    exactly once, before it knows which application the image belongs to, so it never re-reads:
   101	    a statement printed sideways in a batch shows as absent, and the row's note says to check the
   102	    image. Sideways photographs are handled earlier by the general rotation retry (three reads).
   103	    Known approximation in the finder: the column tolerance is half the median line thickness of
   104	    the whole image, which large display type can inflate.
   105	14. **Which findings can fail an application.** Only clear text or numeric differences: a brand the
   106	    label does not carry, alcohol or net contents that disagree with the application, an origin
   107	    statement naming another country, a warning statement that is missing or worded differently.
   108	    Everything else is a review item with its reason and its crop: a class/type worded differently
   109	    from the application's description (labels print the designation the regulations permit, and
   110	    the tool has no table of permitted designations, so a genuinely wrong designation is left to the
   111	    agent); a bottler line naming another party (lawful when bottled for the applicant by another
   112	    permittee); a required value the engine did not read (its read rate on real artwork is about
   113	    80%). Decided against 146 real applications whose registered data had turned every one of them
   114	    into an "issue" (D-041, consult 006).

    64	- Detection rate over the defects the tool assesses: 5/5; 1 planted defects are outside this build's checks and are listed as n/a


===== FILE: docs/EVAL_REAL.md (current text at 3d9bdad, line-numbered) =====
     1	# Evaluation on real approved COLAs
     2	
     3	_Generated by `tools/evaluate_real.py` on 150 approved label applications fetched from TTB's Public COLA Registry by `tools/cola_fetch.py` (applications completed 2026-08-31 to 2026-09-02, 50 per beverage type, fetched 2026-09-03). The images and per-record results stay local (the artwork belongs to the brand owners); this file holds aggregate numbers only._
     4	
     5	_Engine: rapidocr 3.9.2 / onnxruntime 1.29.0; alphabet: printable ASCII (18614 of 18710 classes suppressed); workers: 2._
     6	
     7	## Read this first
     8	
     9	- The registry stores the applicant's uploads, usually flat artwork, often downscaled by the registry (see the image size row). This is the artwork tier of the real world, not the phone-photo tier.
    10	- The COLA form has no alcohol content or net contents fields, so for those only the read rate is reported.
    11	- Class/type is the registry's code description ("STRAIGHT BOURBON WHISKY", "TABLE RED WINE"), not the label's wording ("Kentucky Straight Bourbon Whiskey", "Cabernet Sauvignon"), so the class row understates what the tool would do with the application's actual wording. In the product a class/type that does not match is a review item with the closest text, never an issue (D-041); this row reports the raw text match.
    12	- The applicant is the permit holder; a label may lawfully name a different bottler ("bottled for"), so the applicant row also understates.
    13	- There is no ground truth for the warning statement beyond TTB's approval; the exact rate is what the comparator reports on the registry's image, and the hand-checked cases are listed at the end.
    14	- "Statement located" means the heading was found and a span accumulated behind it, at any similarity; the exact / slips / wording split below it is the accuracy, not the located rate.
    15	- "Country of origin found" is a proxy: the registry's country name matched somewhere on the label at partial-ratio 90 or better, without checking that it sits in an origin statement.
    16	- The alcohol and net-contents read rates count a parse anywhere in the concatenated text of all the record's images, as the extract-only mode of the product does.
    17	- Latency is the service call for the record (decode, all reads, the extra round when it runs); the file read and the pre-fit to the pixel cap are outside it. p95 is the nearest-rank percentile.
    18	- The applicant row runs the product's own bottler check on the registry's full item 8 line (names, address, the name used on the label), exactly as a spreadsheet would carry it (D-041).
    19	- Images above the product's 25 megapixel limit are shrunk to fit before the run; the deployed service refuses them with a message instead.
    20	
    21	| | spirits (n=50) | wine (n=50) | malt (n=50) | all (n=150) |
    22	|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    23	| Brand name: match | 56% | 64% | 48% | 56% |
    24	| Brand name: match or needs review | 86% | 96% | 82% | 88% |
    25	| Class/type (registry description): match | 10% | 0% | 16% | 9% |
    26	| Class/type (registry description): match or needs review | 54% | 8% | 68% | 43% |
    27	| Applicant name: match | 66% | 86% | 58% | 70% |
    28	| Applicant name: match or needs review | 100% | 100% | 100% | 100% |
    29	| Country of origin found (imports only) | 88% | 94% | 100% | 92% |
    30	| Warning statement located (heading found) | 100% | 98% | 98% | 99% |
    31	| Warning exact (of all) | 62% | 62% | 60% | 61% |
    32	| Warning exact (of located) | 62% | 63% | 61% | 62% |
    33	| Warning heading all capitals (of located) | 98% | 100% | 98% | 99% |
    34	| Warning heading measured heavier than the body (of located) | 46% | 59% | 24% | 43% |
    35	| Warning type weight not measured, small print or inconclusive (of located) | 54% | 41% | 78% | 57% |
    36	| Alcohol statement read | 70% | 90% | 84% | 81% |

The statement is printed in capitals and reads "WOMAN SHOULD NOT DRINK" and "BECAUSE OF RISK OF BIRTH DEFECTS", with no final period. | Wording differs: `-women +WOMAN`, `-the`, `-problems. +PROBLEMS`. "WOMAN" and the missing period are treated as slips (review); the missing "the" is a wording issue. | Right. With the 90-degree turn first it was absent, which is why 270 goes first. | Half right, like the bourbon label above: present, too small to read reliably, visibly reported. |
